# Performance and Benchmark results


Performance comparisons are made against the standard library implementation available on the tested platform, including the relevant `libstdc++`, `libc++`, or MSVC STL implementation.

## Meaurements

### `x86-64`

References for all statements are from intel manual [^1] 

* Benchmarks in `x86-64` machines are done using the `PMU` [^2]. in the following fashion:

   * its important to understand what `core`[^3]., `ref`[^4]. and `tsc`[^5] clock measurements means in this context.

1. `__mm_lfence()` for serialization [^6].
2. `RDPMC` read performance monitoring counters.[^3]. [^4]. (we read both `core` and `ref` measurements).
3. `RDTSC` read the timestamp counter (aka `tsc`) [^5].
4. `__mm_lfence()` for serialization [^6].

it should be a perfect `cycle accurate` benchmark counting both `core` cpu cycles and `ref` cycles as well as `tsc` (timestamp clock) cycles


### `ARM64` 

References for all statements are from arm64 manuals [^7] [^8].

* Benchmarks in `aarch64` (ARM64) machines are a bit more tricky if its MacOS, if its linux is very similar to `x86-64`. 

   * its important to understand what `core`[^9]., `ref`[^10]. and `tsc`[^11] clock measurements means in this context.

1. `ISB` for serialization [^12]. 
2. `PMCCNTR` read performance monitoring counter (we read `core` (if allowed)) [^9]
3. `CNTVCT_EL0` read virtual timer (aka `tsc`) [^11]
4. `ISB` for serialization [^12]. 

it should be a perfect `cycle accurate` benchmark if `PMCCNTR` is allowed, counting both `core` cpu cycles `tsc` freq clk ticks.



> Compiling in Release :

## Benchmark Results

Headline values use the **median core cycles** recorded in the benchmark JSON files.

Expand any benchmark cell to inspect the complete recorded mean, median, minimum, p95, and p99 measurements.

**Relative performance** is calculated directly from the recorded measurements as `baseline / Bin2Chars`.

[^1]: [intel architecture and software development manual](https://cdrdv2.intel.com/v1/dl/getContent/671200)
[^2]: Performance Monitoring Unit - this is the hardware module that provides the facilities for monitoring CPU performance. Chapter 22.1 [^1].
[^3]: `core` means `none halted clocktics` - This *measures clock cycles in witch the specified logical processor is not halted and is not in any power saving state*. Chapter 22.7 [^1]. This is measured utilizing `RDPMC` instruction that works in ANY microachitecture. Chapter 22.6.3 [^1].
[^4]: `ref` means *performance events that counts clockticks of a **constant** reference frequency*. Chpater 22.7 [^1]. This is to ensure that individual core boost clocks that deviate from reference are properly accounted when comparing 2 benchmark functions.
[^5]: `tsc` is the *time-stamp counter mechanism that can be used to monitor and identify relative time occurance of processor events*. Measured by `RDTSC` instruction. Chapter 20.17 [^1]
[^6]: `__mm_lfence()` is an instrinsic that allows execution of `LFENCE` instruction; this instruction *performes a **serializing** operation in all load-from-memory that were issued prior to the instruction. 
Specifically, `LFENCE` does not execute until all prior instructions have completed locally, and no later instructions begin execution until `LFENCE` completes. 
Even further, instructions following an `LFENCE` may be fetched from memory before the LFENCE, but they will not execute (even speculatively) until the LFENCE completes*. 
Vol. 2-A 3-552.[1^]. This is excelent for measurements since the fence wont execute until all previous instructions are done making the measurement very presice.

[^7]: [arm64 system register manual](https://support.arm.com/documentation/ddi0487/mc/)
[^8]: [arm64 instruction manual](https://support.arm.com/documentation/ddi0487/mc/)
[^9]: `core` means every processor clock cycle. This is measured from the `PMCCNTR_EL0` instruction with `PMCR_EL0` configured with bit(3) to `0` as specified in page 2608 [^7]. 
Sadly this instruction can be configured to trap and thats exacly what macOS does and wont let you measure real clock cycle perfomance :(. 
Linux can be configured to not trap in the same fashion as in `x86_64` but i dont have a `__arch64__` linux machine.... 
[^10]: `ref`  this is not well defined in aarch64 across vendors so we don't measure it. It will always be 0 for `__arch64__` benchmarks. 
[^11]: `tsc` means the `CNTVCT_EL0` (count virtual timer *el0*) witch measures the ticks in `CNTFRQ_EL0` frequency idependently of core/system frequency. page 1212 [^7] and page 1116[^7]
[^12]: `ISB` *Instruction Serialization Barrier*, this instruction flushes the pipeline in the PE and is a context synchronization event. page 376 [^8] [ISB docs](https://support.arm.com/documentation/ddi0602/2026-06/Base-Instructions/ISB--Instruction-synchronization-barrier-?lang=en)
